web push payloads hard-code `/me` for every role; `SystemJobs.tsx` counts a `partial` run as unhealthy while the watchdog considers it fresh; `monday-digest` keeps a wrapper arm after retirement. *Landed so far:* **The docs and the archive, from the orchestrator.** `PA_DHS_ANNUAL_TRAINING_MATRIX.md` attributed the annual-hour floors to the topic subsections; the floors are in 2600.65(e) and 2800.65(h), which is the same defect `20260904010000` fixed in the two PA templates, and this was the last copy of it. The marketing citations page now says it ranks the most-cited sections rather than indexing every section CareBase governs, and names 2600.64/2800.64 -- the one the crosswalk cites -- as the example, rather than ranking it and claiming something the published data does not support. ARCHITECTURE.md and the ROADMAP deferred-ideas row both said scheduling was not qualification-gated and capped an employee at one shift a calendar day; `enforce_shift_assignment_eligibility` gates it and `20260731053000` dropped the cap. DEPLOYMENT.md said the AI switches default false except voice: three of the six seed true, so a fresh project comes up with three on. The organization export declared whole tables it left out and said nothing about the CLINICAL ROWS it withholds on consent, while README.txt claimed every CSV held the organization's rows -- `20260906290000` moves the consent predicate into one function so a count cannot drift from the filter, and the archive now declares what it withheld, per table, in counts that never name a resident. And the committed user manual was not the bytes the generator produces: it still sold the exclusion screening `20260906020000` removed, and every `pnpm build` dirtied the worktree with a diff nobody could act on, so nobody looked. Regenerated, with a CI step that fails if the release gate regenerates any tracked file (pdf-lib seeds its resource names from a fixed seed, so the output is reproducible). *Fixed:* **Train:** all seven, in `20260906240000` and the frontend. The superseded-record loop was a monthly one: a renewal leaves the old row graded expired for ever, the nightly alert CTE selected every expired row, the alert raised a `training_expired` notification, the stale sweep closed it, and it began again next cycle -- for a requirement the employee had already met. The CTE takes the current record per employee and type now, and the sweep stops requiring a sibling to have reached compliant before it will close a superseded alert. The offline seat clock is the one worth reading twice: it was stamped at sync rather than at study, and the fix was INERT until the second half was found -- `protect_course_progress_timing` overwrites `started_at` with now() on every non-privileged insert, so the clamped value was being discarded. Seat time is load-bearing since `20260906220000` made class hours credit from recorded attendance, so this one under-credited a real learner against a state hour requirement. Offline progress also never carried the block reached, so a comprehensive course's synced 100% could never satisfy the completion rule and the live player wrote the percentage back down. A canceled assignment is refused by name instead of leaking a constraint. The trainer dashboard stopped treating draft as the live state, so opening a class for enrolment stopped removing it from the kiosk on the morning it ran, and a class can be started and cancelled from the product. And the public passport prints recorded credit hours or nothing at all, rather than a course's scheduled duration under the word Verified. **Policy / regulatory / help:** all nine. Auditors stop being offered policy write controls Auditors stop being offered policy write controls that end in a 42501, the knowledge-check author's numbering matches the employee's, an announcement is visible to whoever published it, and archive-then-republish stops restamping a regulatory update's publication date -- which the page's own note already said it would not do. `attest-policy` refuses when it cannot read the version hash instead of recording an attestation to nothing. The help job-aide audience is constrained to real role names, so a typo stops silently hiding an aide. The state-form prefill is the one that grew: there was no precedent to follow, both sibling functions being fuzzy too, so the word sets were replaced with the actual AcroForm field names read out of all four live DHS PDFs -- which found two real traps, a tier that matched the wrong field only because of field order, and an ALR facility field whose name normalises to contain the word resident. It also established two facts as facts: no form has an admission-date field, so that fill was dead code, and the ALR preadmission form has no date of birth. The two items this area had left to a person are both closed now: the citation attribution in `PA_DHS_ANNUAL_TRAINING_MATRIX.md` was corrected by the orchestrator, and `PA_CITATIONS_LAST_VERIFIED` moved 2026-08-04 -> 2026-09-06 on the owner's statement that they read the two published sources -- which is the only thing that may move it. `check-dhs-sources.mjs` now reports both link sets resolving at a human verification age of 0 days, so the weekly workflow no longer goes red around 2026-09-18 for a reading that had been done. **Imports / export / jobs:** seven fixed in `20260906250000` and the frontend, one stale, one half deliberately left out of the repository. A single bad row stranded an entire import: finalize refused while any invalid row remained, and NOTHING in the database could skip one -- worse, re-uploading the same bytes returned the same dead receipt, so the stranded job owned the file. Skip and cancel now exist, and `canceled` (in the status check since the table was created, never once written) releases the checksum. The HRIS decision split in two: nothing calls `stage_hris_import_row` BY DESIGN, since the vendor adapter is deliberately not in this repository, but no tenant could create a source system either, so every one of them opened the tab on "no source configured" with every control disabled behind a row nothing could insert. An invalid HRIS row could also be neither decided nor applied -- a state no decision could clear. A retryable failed export could be requested twice, and Download was offered on an archive whose object had already been purged. Employee numbers are unique per organization now, over non-null values, with the migration naming any offending pairs rather than dying on a bare constraint error. The `current_date` claim is stale and was checked across all 1,089 functions, the views, the constraints and the import workers' date handling rather than assumed. **Platform / billing / dashboards:** all ten, in `20260906250000` and the frontend. The one that needed reproducing before fixing: two Stripe events in the same second could apply out of order, because `event.created` is a UNIX second and the tie-break fell through to comparing random event ids. Reproduced on the local stack with two real events delivered in causal order, which left the tenant in `grace` when it should have been `active`; the guard now orders on the receipt's own verified-at timestamp, and a pgTAP assertion pins that cross-second ordering is unchanged. Three pricing models that never agreed are now one: the worksheet and the emailed model priced a year at twelve monthly payments while the catalog sold an annual price, and the Value Center's estimate never netted the subscription off the value it claimed. The admin dashboard's tiles sum to its own total by construction now, and the organization filter offers every state the column allows. Two migrations that updated a job definition by its cron name instead of its key matched zero rows and are corrected -- and the description was wrong in the other direction too, naming three statements where the wrapper runs four. Web push stops sending every role to the employee home. A `partial` run stops reading as unhealthy when the watchdog considers it fresh. **Identity:** eight fixed in `20260906230000` and the frontend, one stale. `verify-identity-domain` stopped demanding a raw aal2 claim ahead of the policy, which refused a demo tenant's administrator with a message naming a factor nobody had asked them to enrol. Security & Governance stopped labelling every impersonation authorization as its own ending, and the identity entity is selectable at last, so the mfa_reset rows I8 added are visible rather than filtered out. `resend-invitation` asks `auth.users` rather than trusting a ledger nothing writes on acceptance, reconciles it, and refuses in the product's own words instead of handing GoTrue's text to an administrator. The MFA wall carries the deep link through to the security page and back again. A canceled organization says it is canceled and has a way back that actually works -- reinstating the provider state would have been a no-op reported as success, so the control writes a billing override instead. `identity_security_policies` has a UI reading the real row, offering only the one field the CHECK constraint leaves settable. Platform admins can reach the invitation ledger both backends already admitted them to. And `supabase/config.toml` has an `[auth]` block, so local and CI stop running on CLI defaults that contradict the documented posture. The deactivation dialog was already correct. **Guest / public / server:** seven fixed in `20260906230000` and the frontend, four stale, one for counsel. A crafted 36-character safety-report token raised 22P02 inside the gate, and the exception rolled back the failed-attempt row -- so the one shape most worth counting was the one never counted. It parses under an IF now and returns an ordinary denial. The portal home page stopped spending two throttle requests per load, which also exposed a latent bug: the denial test compared against NULL, was never taken, and a refused request went on to read the grant, the requests and the payment link. The survey-packet download stopped short-circuiting a short guess ahead of the gate, which defeated the gate's stated reason for running first. `subscribe-updates` scrubs the one catch that still had the subscriber's address in scope. Seven credential-bearing public routes had no metadata entry at all, so each served the homepage title and canonical with index,follow; they are noindex now, excluded from the sitemap, and the server serves a stem's prerendered head to its tokenised children, a lookup that never matched the URL anyone actually holds. A deploy's new service worker announces itself instead of taking the tab silently, and a lazy-chunk miss no longer reloads over unsaved input. Four findings were already fixed and are recorded as stale. The privacy and terms item is CLOSED: counsel approved the agreement on 2026-09-06, reported by the owner. It was never an engineering question -- naming a legal entity, whether the BAA belongs in the published terms, and which effective date governs are counsel's calls, and counsel has made them. No copy was changed on that approval; if the wording is to move, that is a fresh request with counsel's text. **Integrations:** all six, in `20260906260000`. A command type nothing drains is refused at submission with 422 rather than given a 202 and left `accepted` for ever -- one predicate, `app_private.integration_command_is_consumable`, read by both the acceptor and the drain so they cannot disagree -- and the drain now emits applied/rejected events carrying the error code but never the apply function's message, which is a truncated `sqlerrm` and can quote a row. `GET /v1/commands/:id` exists. Webhook rotation's `previous_*` columns are finally read: the claim returns both secrets inside the fifteen-minute window and the dispatcher signs with both, which is what the UI copy already promised. Dead letters can be replayed, a single subscription can be switched off, an endpoint auto-disables at 25 consecutive failures -- and can be switched back on, which nothing could do before, so every manual deactivation had been permanent. All three voice tools now write an audit row and fail closed if they cannot, because two of them read training, credential and resident-compliance data down a phone line and left nothing. And a squatted identity domain releases: a pending claim, never verified, older than ninety days, with no SSO connection depending on it, passes to the next registrant with the takeover written to the audit log. **Exclusion-screening residue (I32):** four of five closed in `20260906260000`, one stale, one half needing a person. The seeded Help Center FAQ is deleted; the Survey Day entrance-conference prompt is deactivated rather than deleted, because tenants may hold observations against it; the `feature_definitions` copy that sold "background and exclusion screening" into commercial packages is corrected. The compliance requirement keeps its `requirement_key` -- every tenant's resolved profile references it, so renaming is a data migration and not a copy change -- but its label and evidence type now say background check and suitability determination, which is what Pennsylvania actually requires. The `release_cohorts` half is stale: that table is empty, the row was deleted by `20260802030000`, and the description survives only in two deployed migrations. The marketing caption and its narration source are corrected, and the .vtt records that the shipped MP4 audio still says the old line: re-rendering it needs a vendor key and paid credits, so that half is the owner's. **Workforce / scheduling:** nine fixed in `20260906280000` and the frontend, one stale. Employee self-service no longer approves its own absence -- a call-off filed by the employee lands `pending` and reaches the manager queue, while a manager recording one on somebody's behalf still lands approved, because that IS the decision. Acknowledging a shift handoff now takes the same predicate as writing one, so an auditor cannot close a clinical handoff. An expired swap says it is expired, offers rejection and hides approval, instead of sitting in the queue looking live and answering with a bare refusal. Administrator Qualification can be tracked for an org_admin, which is who actually holds the licence in a single-home personal care home; the RLS always allowed it and only the picker did not. The renewal notification sends an employee to their own credentials page rather than a manager-only route, and the same two-line defect in the certification-approval notification is fixed with it. The transfer wizard offers only active facilities that are not the one the employee is already at, and the eligibility explainer takes a searched employee and a facility list rather than two raw UUIDs. `employee_background_check_profiles.facility_id` was already realigned on transfer by `20260906070000` -- stale, verified against the live trigger rather than assumed. **Frontend / modules:** three fixed, one stale, one NOT fixed and worth the owner's attention. Applying a lifecycle case, editing an employee and completing a course now invalidate the matrix, schedule and dashboard roots, so none of them serve a stale answer for the sixty-second window. The sidebar marks the three pages a facility manager can only read. Train-only access to Documents was already fixed by `0487ca2` and was checked by executing the predicate rather than reading it. The search entry to Report-an-event was deliberately left admitting auditors, because the concurrent fix to that page turns its two locked doors into review links -- hiding it would now strand a working entry point. **The bundle budget is closed, by the procedure the gate itself documents.** Measured 4793.8 KiB, 92.2%, and CSS at 90.7% and the initial shell at 91.1% were over the same warning line with no row of their own. `scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs` says in its own header what to do here: these budgets are regression tripwires rather than ledgers, the warning band exists so a budget is raised deliberately on main instead of failing feature branches, and the warning text says to raise it. Five previous raises are recorded inline the same way, including the H3 one this finding compares itself to. So the work was proving it is growth and not a regression, which is what the budget is for: zero modules duplicated across the 424 emitted chunks, zero duplicated long string literals, zero inlined data URIs, every per-route budget still passing, and the entry chunk at 88.7% of its own. Three build-level levers were measured and rejected with their numbers so nobody retries them -- two came out LARGER, and the third saved 33 KiB while pushing the entry chunk and the shell over budget. Raised 5200 to 5900, 190 to 210 and 1250 to 1320, each with that evidence written where the next person will read it. The initial shell got the stricter reading it deserves as a per-page-load guardrail: +3.9% raw across the whole program's history, about ten kilobytes compressed on a first load, and F5's finding that the entry chunk is the app shell rather than anything liftable still stands. **Safety / incidents / survey:** all nine, in `20260906270000` and the frontend. The 48-hour written report is modelled the way the existing notification rules are, derived from the `state_hotline` rows so the two lists cannot drift, and it inherits the whole existing machinery -- anchor, re-anchor on a reversal, overdue alert, outstanding count. `verify_work_order` stops overwriting the deadline it had just computed correctly and calls the recalculation instead. The due-soon ladder is derived from each item's own cadence rather than a flat thirty days, so a monthly drill stops reading "due soon" for most of the month, and nothing on a cadence of two months or more moves. "Mark Notified" is refused and no longer offered on a notification recorded as not required. A corrective action cannot jump from cancelled to completed. The incident page shows the follow-up item's due date it already had. The QAPI pickers offer only what the RPCs accept, and Report-an-event stops sending an auditor at two doors that are locked for them. The fire-drill tracker prints passing drills and the monthly verdict on the clock's own rule, rather than a count that read as a drill having been held. **Resident care:** one fixed, one stale. The offline refusal after a discharge told the aide "someone else documented this while your device was offline", which is not what happened: the guard raises the same SQLSTATE as an unrelated one, so the ladder fell through to the wrong arm. It now checks the resident first and carries the guard's own sentence, which names them. The unpaginated compliance report is stale -- that page already reads a paged hook. The residual found while checking: the other consumer of the same data was still unpaged, and paging it stops every resident list in the product truncating silently at a thousand rows. |

The console stopped inferring "can this kill switch stop the job" from the cron command string and reads `claims_before_running`, which is what `claim_system_job_execution` actually consults; `kill_switch_is_honest.test.sql` pins the column rather than the string (`20260906170000`). **I29 (4):** closed by the invitation half of `0487ca2`. `record_user_invitation_sent` reopens the ledger row and `invite-user` passes `p_is_active: true` on both the profile and the employee path, so a re-invite produces an account that can sign in rather than one that is still deactivated. **G27:** closed by J56. The three seeded governed templates and every installed version carry `crosswalkObligationId: "staff-training"`, so the crosswalk can find the rule whose status it reports (`20260906150000`). **I7:** corrected in place. The row body opened its last paragraph with "why this row stays open" while the status column said `done`; it now says which half is done and why the residual is not something a migration can close. **H13:** answered in place, in part. `Maple Grove Senior Living` is a `supabase/seed.sql` fixture, not a customer, so the question the row asks has no third answer -- what is left is an owner decision about production data, which is not made from a repository. **H14:** closed. DEPLOYMENT.md said the AI kill switches default false except voice; three of the six seed true (`ai_course_generation_enabled` and `ai_video_generation_enabled` in `20260706043635`, alongside voice in `20260724220000`), so a fresh project comes up with three on. The doc now lists all six with the migration that seeds each. **I8:** stale. The desktop users table already carries the reset-MFA control, and the line that added it records this exact defect -- it used to exist only under 768px, so the lost-device recovery drill had no control at all on any viewport at or above md. Landed in `0487ca2`. **I10:** closed by the safety pass. A 48-hour written-report deadline is now modelled, derived from the existing `state_hotline` notification rules so the two lists cannot drift, and it inherits the machinery those rules already had: the anchor on when the facility knew, the re-anchor when a reportability determination is reversed, the overdue alert and the outstanding count on the incident page (`20260906270000`). What is still not modelled is a work item, and that is deliberate -- no notification type has one, and inventing a mechanism for this row alone is a bigger decision than the row. **I5:** fixed by routing the import worker through `import_apply_resident_contact` rather than by removing its unused grant. Removing the grant would have deleted one grant and left the UNVALIDATED direct write as the only path, and `service_role` keeps blanket DML on the table regardless, so nothing real would have been narrowed. Through the RPC both processors enforce the contact-type vocabulary, the required name, the resident-in-organization check, and derive `facility_id` from the resident instead of trusting the browser-written ledger payload. **I13:** fixed, and sharper than the row recorded. A delivery finalized as `unknown` was written `failed`, so the console already showed a Retry button on it and the RPC refused every press -- a control that could only produce an error toast, over a row nothing could ever clear. Retry now accepts an ambiguous outcome finalized more than six hours ago with no provider event since, and the button appears only when it will work. The synthetic-check unknown counter is bounded to 24 hours; unbounded, one 15-second timeout wrote a durable failed run against the health job for ever. **I23:** settled. The `current_date` half is stale, and was checked rather than assumed -- across all 1,089 functions in `public` and `app_private`, the views and the constraints, and then again for the same defect under other names (`now()::date`, `localtimestamp`, a sliced ISO string in the import workers). Zero hits. The impersonation-banner half was closed by J13, and the P3 items the row points at are J74, which is done. **I18:** stale, and checked against the live bodies rather than the files. Both defects item (3) named are gone on this branch: the entrance-conference item types travel from the seeded row through the Survey Day snapshot into the workspace payload, and the readiness verdict is one shared module whose header names this exact drift and whose rule is that nothing on file is not readiness. Landed in `0487ca2`. **I11:** stale. Both extensions landed earlier in this pass, in `20260906090000`: the `violation:<id>` work-item creator anchors on the plan-of-correction date rather than a flat seven days, and `update_work_item_assignment` clears `escalated_at` so a reassigned item is not still escalated against the person who left it. **I23 (the `current_date` half):** stale, and checked rather than assumed. No function in `public` or `app_private` contains a word-bounded `current_date` today -- `20260727010100` converted the whole schema to `public.pa_today()`, `apply_hris_import_batch` included. The row's other halves stand. |
| J76 | An org_admin can extend their own trial indefinitely and flip `is_demo` with one direct table write | S | done | **P0, Entitlements / tenancy** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). The UPDATE policy lets an org_admin write their own `organizations` row from the browser (Settings uses it for `ai_features_enabled`), and the only column guards are the subscription quartet and the BAA pair. Nothing guards `trial_ends_at`, `is_demo`, `demo_seed_version`, `demo_reset_at`, `plan_name` or `slug`. Setting `trial_ends_at = '2099-01-01'` makes every module resolve entitled for ever with no subscription; setting `is_demo = true, demo_seed_version = 1` switches off mandatory MFA for every privileged role in the tenant, passes the BAA gate for AI without a BAA, makes the daily demo re-seed start inserting Sunrise fixtures into a real tenant, and drops every provider notification. `org_baa_gated_ai.test.sql:109-125` proves the write path is live; no test names either column in an UPDATE guard. `organizations` is the only tenant-writable input to the entitlement decision, and it is open. *Where:* `organizations_update` (`20260704050042:52-56`, admits org_admin); `protect_organization_subscription_fields` (`20260813181000:119-134`, reverts only `subscription_status`, `package_id`, `max_facilities`, `max_users`); `ensure_trial_has_an_end_date` (`20260904070000`, BEFORE INSERT only); `get_effective_entitlements` (`20260724180000:69-77`); `get_my_mfa_policy` (`20260729130000:36-46`); `org_ai_allowed` (`20260725000000`); `restore_demo_baseline`, `suppress_demo_notification_delivery` (`20260717163659`) *Fix:* Extend `protect_organization_subscription_fields` to revert `trial_ends_at`, `is_demo`, `demo_seed_version`, `demo_reset_at`, `plan_name` and `slug` unless `is_platform_admin()` or `app.privileged_write = 'on'`; add the assertion to `org_baa_gated_ai.test.sql`. Verified by the lead reviewer against the source. *Known:* none (H7/H13 are about a null `trial_ends_at`; neither notices the column is tenant-writable) *Fixed:* `20260906060000` extends `protect_organization_subscription_fields` to revert `trial_ends_at`, `is_demo`, `demo_seed_version`, `demo_reset_at`, `plan_name` and `slug` as well as the subscription quartet. `is_platform_admin()` and `app.privileged_write` remain the two escape hatches. |
| J77 | "Suspend Organization" is silently reversed by the tenant's next Stripe event, and "Reactivate" always writes `active` | S-M | done | **P1, Billing / tenancy** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). The admin page writes `organizations.subscription_status = 'suspended'` and nothing else; `billing_accounts.state_source` becomes `manual_suspension` only at INSERT, so the webhook's preserve branch (`billing_state = 'suspended' and state_source = 'manual_suspension'`) is never true for a UI-suspended tenant, and both the subscription and the invoice branches end by overwriting `organizations.subscription_status` from `billing_accounts`. A tenant suspended for cause is reactivated by its own monthly `invoice.paid` with no audit row that says so. Reactivate writes a literal `active` whatever the prior state and never touches `billing_accounts`, so the list says active while entitlements still read the account. *Where:* `src/pages/admin/OrganizationDetail.tsx:91-114`; `ensure_organization_billing_account` (`20260711200648:583-606`, AFTER INSERT only); `process_stripe_billing_event` (`20260905210000:378,505-543,600-634`) *Fix:* Make suspend/reactivate an RPC that writes `billing_accounts` (`suspended` / `manual_suspension` / reason) and restores the provider-derived state on reactivation; make the webhook's org sync leave a suspension alone unless the account row is being unsuspended. Verified by the lead reviewer against the source. *Known:* none (the `canceled` shown as suspended item in section 4.3 is a different defect) *Fixed:* `20260906060000` adds `set_organization_suspension`, and `/admin/organizations/:id` now calls it with a required reason instead of writing `organizations.subscription_status` alone. |
| J78 | `kill_switch_can_stop_job` is inverted for every Edge-cron job: the console says the switch is dead and the watchdog pages a job the operator turned off | S | done | **P2, Jobs control plane** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). I17 reasoned that the switch is reachable only through `execute_registered_sql_job`, but every Edge worker calls `claim_system_job_execution` first and returns 500 when the claim raises, so the switch stops all 43 claimed jobs; only the watchdog is exempt. With the predicate false for the 14 Edge jobs, the card says "Disable will not stop this job" on notification dispatch and certificate PDFs (it does), and the watchdog keeps monitoring anything whose switch "cannot act", so disabling a critical Edge job during a provider outage pages every five minutes -- the problem I17 fixed, facing the other way. The test pins the wrong fact. *Where:* `app_private.kill_switch_can_stop_job` (`20260905150000:65`, true only for wrapper-routed cron commands); `claim_system_job_execution` (`20260711162509:597`, raises on `kill_switch_enabled`); every Edge worker claims before working (14 functions); `run_system_job_watchdog` (`20260906020000`); `src/pages/admin/SystemJobs.tsx:362-366`; `kill_switch_is_honest.test.sql:26-32` *Fix:* Make the predicate true for every definition whose worker claims (false only for the watchdog or a future no-claim definition); retarget the two assertions. Verified by the lead reviewer against the source. *Known:* I17 (`done`) -- its premise is wrong on this branch; STATUS-WRONG for that half *Fixed:* `20260906170000` records `claims_before_running` on `system_job_definitions` and makes `kill_switch_can_stop_job` read it, instead of inferring the answer from the cron command string. The switch is checked inside `claim_system_job_execution`, and every Edge worker claims before it works, so it stops all forty-three claiming jobs; the watchdog is the one deliberate exemption. `kill_switch_is_honest.test.sql` now pins that fact rather than the cron string. |
| J79 | Nobody can opt out of product email: `profiles.email_opt_out` has no writer in the product and outbound mail carries no unsubscribe mechanism | S | done | **P2, Notifications** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). The pipeline honours `email_opt_out` but the only path that sets it is the SendGrid consent webhook, which fires only if SendGrid injects an unsubscribe link the payload never asks for. An employee who wants CareBase to stop emailing them has no control, with or without login. SMS (STOP) and the newsletter are fine. *Where:* `update_profile_contact_preferences` (`20260905210000:38-135`, no email parameter); `src/pages/auth/NotificationSettings.tsx:117`; `supabase/functions/dispatch-notifications/index.ts:110-121` (no `asm`, no subscription tracking); `record_notification_consent_event` (`20260714233041`, the only writer, fed by SendGrid events) *Fix:* Add `p_email_opt_out` to the preferences RPC and a toggle on NotificationSettings; set a SendGrid ASM group so `List-Unsubscribe` exists for recipients who never log in. Verified by the lead reviewer against the source. *Known:* none *Fixed:* `20260906110000` adds `p_email_opt_out` to `update_profile_contact_preferences` -- the first writer of that column a person can reach -- and Notification Settings gained the toggle. Turning email off needs nobody's permission; turning it back on has to come from the recipient, the same asymmetry the SMS consent rule applies. |
| J80 | The Dashboard's "Overall compliance" and the Reports "Compliance Summary" score disagree by construction; the platform tiles count demo tenants and terminated staff | M | done | **P2, Dashboards / reports** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). The dashboard dedupes to the current record per employee and type, unions practicums, and has no sandbox exclusion or date window; the report counts every training record row (a renewed training contributes its old expired row and its new compliant one), excludes sandbox facilities and applies a due-date window. Neither excludes terminated employees' records while the dashboard's employee count is active-only. Platform side, `orgsByStatus` counts demo tenants, so the Sunrise tenant inflates "Trial Accounts" and the readiness counters. *Where:* `get_org_dashboard_summary` (`20260727010100:3905-3945`); `generate_paged_compliance_report` (`20260727020000:1463-1482`); `get_platform_health` (`20260730140000:19-24,86-91`); `src/pages/admin/AdminDashboard.tsx:56-61,385-388` *Fix:* Have the report's compliance summary use the dashboard's current-record CTE (or a shared view), state its filters in the header, and exclude demo organizations from the platform tiles. | J81 | A server-side idle or kiosk lock is displayed as "Organization Access Suspended" with only a sign-out button | S | done | **P2, Identity / shell** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). MainLayout decides "suspended" from whether the own `organizations` row is readable, before the idle-lock gate mounts; `current_org_id()` is also null while the session is locked server-side, so a user whose session locked on idle and who reloads the tab gets the suspension screen and the unlock prompt never renders. Workaround: sign out and in. *Where:* `src/components/layout/MainLayout.tsx:112-153`; `src/hooks/useOrganizations.ts:63-78`; `current_org_id()` / `current_session_unlocked()` (`20260716224753`) *Fix:* Resolve the lock first (`get_current_idle_session_lock` before the suspension probe), or read suspension through a definer that ignores the session lock. | J82 | "Run now" on the System job watchdog always fails and records a false failed run | S | done | **P2, Jobs control plane** (from the platform / billing / notifications reviewer, whose report landed after the first commit). `request_system_job_rerun` refuses only `retry_mode = 'none'`, so the watchdog's queued run is created, the wrapper raises 22023 and a durable failed run is written against the one job designed to be un-switchable -- the same shape as I17's five missing keys, one key over. *Where:* `src/pages/admin/SystemJobs.tsx:406-413`; definition `20260726250000:136-139` (`retry_mode 'automatic'`); `run-system-job/index.ts:163-172` (no `EDGE_JOBS` entry); `execute_registered_sql_job` (`20260905240000:66-135`, no watchdog arm) *Fix:* Add a wrapper arm for `system-job-watchdog` or register it `retry_mode = 'none'`. Reported by the area reviewer with quoted lines; not independently re-read. *Known:* I17/H20 fixed other keys; this one is not recorded *Fixed:* `20260906170000` registers the watchdog `retry_mode = 'none'`, which is what it is: it runs on its own cron, it is deliberately exempt from the kill switch, and a manual re-run of a watchdog is not a meaningful action. `request_system_job_rerun` now refuses it instead of creating a queued run that the wrapper raises on and writes back as a durable failed run. |
| J83 | A failed lookup inside a CSV importer is read as a missing record, so the row is reported as unknown or applied as a duplicate | S | done | **P2, Imports.** Raised by the automated review on PR #494, then found to be wider than the three lines it flagged. Supabase returns `{ data, error }`, and for the two read shapes an importer leans on -- a `.maybeSingle()` lookup and a plain list select -- a failure and an empty result are the same value: `data` is null and the error sits in the half nobody read. So an RLS denial, a misconfigured client or a dropped connection arrived wearing the costume of "there is no such record". Three consequences, all silent: the resident and employee lookups answered `Unknown resident_external_id` / `Unknown employee_number`, sending the operator to fix a CSV that was never wrong; every duplicate check (resident, contact, credential, room, draft assessment, training record) read as "no match" and applied the row as a CREATE, producing a second copy of a record that already existed -- the duplication several of these files carry comments about preventing; and the `data_import_rows` receipt load fed the map that makes a resumed import skip rows it already applied, so one transient failure emptied that map and the resume re-applied everything it had already written. *Where:* 23 sites across all eight `supabase/functions/bulk-import-*/index.ts`. *Closed by:* an explicit refusal at each site (500 with the underlying message and the `job_id`, so the operator resumes rather than re-uploads), and `scripts/check-import-lookup-errors.mjs` in `check:all`, which fails on all 23 pre-fix sites and passes on the fixed tree. `.single()` is deliberately outside the gate: it errors on no-row, so the null check after it refuses either way -- it fails closed, which is the whole difference. The invariant was not invented after the fact: `bulk-import-credentials`, `-employees` and `-training-records` already refused on `ledgerLoadError`; five importers never got that line and eight never got it on their row lookups. |
| J84 | Nine defects the automated reviews found in this pass's own work | M | done | **P1-P2, mixed.** The second automated review on PR #494, read out and verified against the code rather than taken on its word; all nine were real and all nine are fixed, with a pgTAP file (`review_findings_2026_09_06.test.sql`) that fails four of seven assertions against the pre-fix definitions. **P1s.** (1) *Voice.* `VOICE_MAX_SESSION_SECONDS` at the documented 3600 ceiling needed a token with 3660s left -- the session plus the pending-ticket TTL -- and no Supabase token ever has that, so the largest documented setting made browser voice unstartable and the client's forced refresh walked into the same wall. The ceiling is now derived from the token hour minus that handoff, and a test asserts the property rather than the number. (2) *Compliance clocks.* `rederive_resident_compliance_due_dates` joined `resident_compliance_rule_packs` straight into an `UPDATE ... FROM`, matching both a tenant's override and the platform row; with two matching source rows Postgres takes one arbitrarily, and in practice it took the platform offset the tenant had deliberately replaced. Now the same `distinct on (item_type) ... organization_id nulls last` precedence `instantiate_resident_compliance_items` uses. (3) *Training authorization.* `grant_additional_quiz_attempt` and `cancel_course_assignment` are SECURITY DEFINER and checked only `assert_content_permission(organization_id, 'training.sessions.manage')`, which every facility_manager and trainer holds organization-wide -- so `course_assignments_update`'s `is_assigned_to_facility(facility_id)` never ran and an assignment id was the only thing between a manager at one site and a learner at another. (4) *Resident funds.* `post_resident_personal_fund_transaction` never looked at `resident_personal_fund_account_closures`; settlement does not change the account row, so deposits, withdrawals and adjustments could still be posted after the final disbursement and leave a closed append-only ledger with a new nonzero balance. The other three writers were read and are sound. (5) *Policy attestations.* Publishing a new version stamps `superseded_at` and leaves `status` as `pending` on purpose, and `attest-policy` only ever asked about `status` -- so an employee holding the old link could still sign replaced text, the exact stale evidence J7 exists to refuse. Now refused on the read and filtered on the service-role update, which is where the publish-in-between race lives. `useAttestPolicy` also unwraps the refusal body the way `useProfiles` does: it rethrew the bare FunctionsHttpError, so every sentence this function has taken trouble over -- the unreadable fingerprint, the unpassed knowledge check, and now the superseded version -- reached the employee as "returned a non-2xx status code". (6) *Notifications.* Turning email off flipped the preferred channel to `web_push`, which the save guard rejects without a subscription, and switching back to `email` hit a guard on the pairing the RPC deliberately allows -- so in the default configuration the J79 opt-out could not be saved at all. **P2s.** (7) The session approval preview still multiplied `duration_hours` after J25 changed approval to credit each attendee's seat time capped by the schedule, so the card promised hours approval would not write; the preview now mirrors the server's rule and reports no per-attendee figure when they differ. (8) `annualTrainingHours` sliced the leading ten characters off `course_completion_credits.credited_at` -- a timestamptz PostgREST serialises in UTC -- and compared that to a facility window, so a course credited on a Pennsylvania evening fell into the wrong training year; `facilityDateOf` in `dateUtils` is the fix and the tool for the next one. (9) `/admin/qualified-workforce` is platform-admin-only and passed `user.organizationId`, which is null for exactly that role, so the HRIS card reported "select an organization" and offered nothing to select on the only screen where a source can be registered; it now follows the header's "Viewing as" org. *The wider sweep of that last one is J85.* |
| J85 | Every other place an instant was sliced into a calendar date | S-M | done | **P2, Dates.** The sweep J84 deferred, asked for directly. Seventeen truncation sites were read and each one's source column resolved against the live schema rather than its name: **ten were real** and are fixed with `facilityDateOf`, **seven were correct** and are left alone with the reasoning recorded. Real: `submitted_at` on a PoC version, the care header's `asOf`, a conflicting record's `at`, a change signal's `windowEnd` (`now.toISOString()` -- while its `windowStart`, a facility-day boundary, was already right), a signal entry's `at`, a resident card's `since`, both hospital-episode stamps, an attestation's `attested_at`, and `documents.created_at` seeding a completion-date field -- that last one *writes*, and after 20:00 ET it seeded tomorrow, a date several of these RPCs refuse outright. The one behavioural defect was `plan.effective_date >= episode.return_time.slice(0,10)`: a DATE compared against a UTC-sliced timestamptz, so a support plan written the evening a resident came back from hospital was judged to predate the return and the conflict that guard suppresses fired anyway. Correct and untouched: `scheduleDates.isoDate` and `calendarExport.addOneDay` (UTC arithmetic on a bare date string built at `T00:00:00Z` -- they never ask what day it is now), `isDateOverdue` (deliberately split from `isInstantOverdue`, and both its callers pass DATE columns), two slices of `resident_support_plans.effective_date` (a `date`, so the slice is a no-op), and a `payload_sha256` prefix that is not a date at all. *The helper needed hardening first:* `facilityDateOf` now returns a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` unchanged, because several of these call sites are fed by a union of a date column and a timestamptz, and converting a date column means `new Date("2026-03-01")` -- UTC midnight, 28 February in Pennsylvania -- so a helper written only for instants would have walked every date value back a day at exactly the sites that needed it most. *Closed by:* the ten fixes plus a second half added to `check-date-only-parsing.mjs` rather than a new gate, on that file's own instruction; it derives the timestamptz column set it already had, fires on six of the ten pre-fix sites, and its docblock states plainly which four it cannot see and why a name-based scan is a floor under the reading rather than a substitute for it. |
| J86 | Two defects in the J84 fixes themselves | S | done | **P2, Training.** A third review round, requested deliberately because the previous two had each found real P1s and the last three commits had been read by nobody. Both findings were real. **(1) The facility guard restated half a policy while claiming to restate all of it.** `grant_additional_quiz_attempt` and `cancel_course_assignment` gained `is_assigned_to_facility(v_assignment.facility_id)` in J84 -- but that is a READ predicate, true for an `auditor` at every facility in their organization, while `course_assignments_update` admits only org_admin, facility_manager and trainer. So an auditor holding an effective `training.sessions.manage` grant passed the guard. No builtin role template grants an auditor that permission, so it was not a live hole -- but the whole value of restating a policy inside a SECURITY DEFINER function is that it keeps holding when the grants around it change, and the comment asserting equivalence was simply wrong. Both RPCs now check the role list and the facility, in that order, with distinct refusals. Two pgTAP assertions added; both pass against the fixed definitions and fail against the J84 ones. **(2) The approval preview promised scheduled hours again through its error path.** When `useTrainingAttendanceEvidence` fails, the evidence map is empty, every attendee falls to the scheduled-hours fallback, and the card printed those figures under a sentence reading "The hours above are still what approval will write" -- which stopped being true when J25 moved crediting to seat time. Approval reads the stored `seat_minutes` and would write less. The numeric preview and the flagged list are now both suppressed in that state, replaced by a sentence saying the credit cannot be shown and why. Same species as the J84 finding it sits beside: a stale sentence describing behaviour that had been replaced underneath it. |
| J87 | Five more, from a fourth review round | S-M | done | **P2, mixed.** Requested on a green head specifically because rounds two and three had each found defects in the round before, and asked for a sceptical read of the two things this pass keeps getting wrong: SQL authorization guards, and copy that asserts what the backend does. Both categories came back with something. **Authorization, in the opposite direction to J86.** `verify_corrective_action` called `assert_incident_manager`, which admits only org_admin and facility_manager -- but `corrective_actions_update` also admits a facility-scoped *trainer* for an action backed by an inspection event or a violation, and `InspectionItemDetail` offers trainers the verify dialog. Every submission from that offered workflow returned 42501. J86 was a guard too wide; this was the same guard shape too narrow, and both were a SECURITY DEFINER function reproducing part of a policy rather than all of it. **Copy, for the third round running.** The attendance dialog's status line still told the recorder "approval credits N h regardless" and, on a zero-length entry, that approval "would still credit the full scheduled hours" -- both describing the rule J25 replaced, at the moment the recorder signs the evidence. J84 fixed the preview copy and J86 fixed the error-path copy; this was the third sentence in the same component. **A defect in J86's own fix.** Hiding the credit preview on `isError` left the *pending* state showing the same overstated figures, because until the query succeeds the evidence map is empty and every attendee falls to the scheduled-hours branch. Now keyed on `attendedRegistrationIds.length > 0 && !isSuccess`, since the hook is disabled when there is nothing to fetch and would otherwise never resolve. **Cross-tenant listing.** `useHrisSourceSystems` took no organization, and `hris_source_systems_select` lets a platform admin read every tenant -- so the card J84 wired to the header's "Viewing as" organization for *registration* still listed every customer's sources, in both it and the run-start picker. Now filtered and keyed on the selected organization. **A wrong reason given to a user.** The voice tool dispatcher treated 403 as an expired sign-in; `voice-tools` answers 401 for that and 403 for an inactive profile, a role no longer permitted, or a failed lookup. A deactivated user was told to sign in again -- the one action that could not help. Both still end the session, which is right; only the sentence differed, and a test named "treats 403 the same way" was pinning the defect and is now inverted. |
| J88 | Seven defects a fifth review round found, one claim it got wrong, and an audit of the class it did not find | M | done | **P1-P2, mixed.** Requested on a green head naming the two patterns rounds three and four kept producing. It came back with eight, seven real. **Verified wrong, and not changed:** the claim that `least(due_date, p_due_date)` yields null for an undated assignment. PostgreSQL's `least` IGNORES nulls -- checked against the live database, `least(null::date, date '2026-01-01')` returns `2026-01-01` -- so the reused assignment is already pulled to the plan-of-correction deadline. **The one that mattered most was mine to answer for:** the J25 guard refused approval whenever any attended registration had zero seat minutes, but the old roster card recorded check-in == check-out on *every* attendance -- that is the defect J25 exists to fix -- and `training_attendance_evidence` is append-only, one row per registration, with no update path. Every session recorded before this branch would have been permanently unapprovable, and nothing an operator could do would fix it. A guard nobody can satisfy is a dead end with a good reason attached. The refusal moved to `record_training_attendance`, which is where the bad row is born and which had never validated the times it derives `seat_minutes` from -- so a direct caller could write one whatever the card did. Approval now falls back to the scheduled hours for a row with no usable seat time: the rule it was recorded under, and the only defensible estimate. Crediting it zero would have stripped hours from people who did attend. **The rest:** a bed reserved through `start_move_in_workspace` holds the PROSPECT's id, and J3's release predicate compared it to the resident's, so the two never matched and discharging a reserved resident left the bed held for somebody not coming; superseded attestations counted as both satisfied evidence and permanent gaps in `regulatoryCrosswalk`, `pchAlrOperationalSnapshot` and the status badge -- the same rows wrong in opposite directions at once; the offline `startedAt` floor came from the oldest manifest for the course version on that device, so a learner who had downloaded it for a previous assignment could backdate past a new annual assignment's elapsed-time gate -- the floor is now the oldest live manifest issued at or after the assignment, and where no such download exists it falls back to `now()` rather than to the assignment date, because a learner who cannot evidence a download losing the offline session's start credit is the safe end of that trade and `assigned_at` as a floor would have let anyone with a registered device backdate to their assignment date, which the online path's `protect_course_progress_timing` refuses outright; two pgTAP assertions pin both halves, and the second (`a bundle downloaded before this assignment existed buys no seat time against it`) fails against the pre-fix definition; taking over an expired domain claim left `created_at` untouched, so a third organization could transfer it again immediately and repeat that indefinitely; `generate_resident_service_tasks` was called with a null requirement id from a SECURITY DEFINER plan activation, walking every active requirement in every tenant; and three more comments still described the crediting rule J25 replaced -- the fourth round running for that, so the prose was swept again after this row's own guard moved. **The audit this round did not need to find:** all 31 definer functions in this branch were compared against the write policies of the tables they mutate, resolving roles through `assert_*` helpers. Six pairs diverged and none is a defect -- three are `cmd=ALL` policies whose USING clause mixes read roles into a write comparison, `set_organization_suspension` is deliberately platform-admin-only, and `decide_shift_swap` uses the permission-based Phase 3 model rather than role names. So the J86/J87 class does not recur. The audit script is deliberately not a gate: it would need allowlists for all three of those shapes, and a gate that noisy teaches people to ignore it. |

| J90 | The J88 migration would not apply, and no local check could have said so | S | done | **P1, Tooling / Resident care.** CI's `database` job -- the one that replays all 678 migrations from empty -- went red on `fa03d83` with `syntax error at or near "active" (SQLSTATE 42601)` in `20260906080000`, and stayed red through two further pushes because I read the local suite and not the job. The J88 scoping fix wrote `''''active''''` inside a `v_new` string. The idiom nests quoting exactly two levels -- `v_new := '...'` is a plpgsql string literal, so apostrophes double once, and the text it emits lands inside `AS $function$ ... $function$`, which `pg_get_functiondef` always dollar-quotes, so nothing doubles again. Four quotes emitted `''active''`: an empty string, a bare word, another empty string. The neighbouring statements in the same block, written earlier, use two. **The interesting part is why nothing caught it.** Not one of the sixteen gates reads inside a `do $do$` block; `supabase db lint` reads function bodies but only ones that already exist; and the local stack -- which had the branch applied and had been kept current with targeted live patches all session -- was the worst possible witness, because the patched function was already correct in the database while the file that produces it was not. The only check that would have spoken is a replay from empty, which is what CI does and what I had stopped doing locally. **Closed by:** the quoting fix, a `db reset` replaying the whole chain (all 678 apply; the full 186-file pgTAP suite then passes against migrations rather than live patches), and `scripts/check-migration-quote-nesting.mjs` in `check:all`. That gate flags a four-quote run adjacent to a word character and deliberately does not flag one bounded by punctuation, because `coalesce(x, '''')` is the correct way to write an empty string literal at that level and this repo has two of them -- so it needs no allowlist. It fires on all four pre-fix sites and passes on the fixed tree. Roughly thirty migrations on this branch use the same patching idiom, so the hazard is structural rather than a one-off. |

Both now carry `current_role() = 'employee'`; the helper is left alone because its four other users are SELECT policies, where an auditor reading is the job. **Money.** `close_resident_personal_fund_account` validated only that the settlement was not in the future. Dated before an existing entry it landed *behind* it in an append-only ledger that nothing can recompute, so the printed ending balance was that later row's while the closure recorded the money returned; and its receipt document was accepted on the FK alone, so a settlement could cite another resident's -- or another tenant's -- evidence from a definer path with RLS off. Both guards already existed on `post_resident_personal_fund_transaction` and are now the same guards, with the same errcodes. **Three more dead ends of the kind this pass keeps making.** A `written_report` rule inserted with no backfill left every reportable incident already on file owing nothing -- no deadline, no alert, no stage -- because presets are generated at insert and determination and the hourly job only updates rows that exist; the backfill is deliberately scoped to OPEN incidents, since minting a 48-hour duty whose deadline passed months ago is the unclearable-task defect this pass has now undone twice. Cancelling a course assignment never reached the copy on the learner's device: `sync_offline_learning_action` checked ownership and never asked the assignment's state, so the learner kept studying, kept being told each checkpoint synced, and found out at completion. And the import centre offered Cancel while a worker held a live claim, which `cancel_data_import_job` refuses by name. **The offline clamp twice more.** `coalesce(v_progress.started_at, ...)` treated any existing progress row as authoritative, so a learner who opened the live player after reconnecting -- the ordinary thing to do -- had their offline hours discarded; `least` is the right function and ignores nulls, so the first-sync case is unchanged. And qualifying manifests on `expires_at > now()` threw away a bundle that was valid when it was STUDIED but expired before the learner got a connection back, erasing the whole session; the test is now against the claimed study time. **And a trainer with nowhere to go.** The credential-renewal and qualification notifications choose their link from the recipient's role, but `trainer` fell to the manager-facing page that `CREDENTIAL_ROLES` and `employee_credentials_select` both exclude them from, while the self-service page was gated to `employee` alone -- so a trainer holding an employee record had no page in the product showing their own approved clearance. The RLS policy already admits them through `owns_employee()`, so the route guard was the only thing in the way. **And one the round did not find, checked for because the round had just named the class.** Having fixed the trainer's credential link in exactly one place, I went looking for the rest of that surface rather than waiting to be told -- which is the check I had failed three times. `grant_additional_quiz_attempt`, added by this pass, notifies the profile behind an employee row and hard-coded `/me/trainings`: employee-only, so every facility_manager, trainer or auditor holding an employee record was sent to a route that redirects them away, and wrong by content too, since that page reads `employee_training_records` while the message is about a course assignment and its quiz. `/me/courses` is both `ANY_ROLE` and the page that lists assignments, so this one needed the right route rather than a wider one. *Closed by:* eight migrations edited in place, five frontend files, one edge function, and coverage that fails against every definition it replaces -- four pgTAP assertions on the handoff guards (both paths), four on settlement, four on the offline clamp, three vitest cases on the crosswalk and the route contract, and three Deno cases on the intake classifier. |

| J92 | Ten more, from a seventh review round | M | done | **P1-P2, mixed.** Requested on a green head, with the findings-per-round series stated in the ask -- 3, 9, 2, 5, 8, 12 -- because the rate had risen and pretending otherwise would have been the more comfortable read. It returned ten. **One was mine, made while fixing a finding of the same class.** J91 relaxed the settlement RPC's future cap to `greatest(now() + 1 day, latest_entry)` so an account holding a far-future entry stays closable; the client blocker still refused anything past 24 hours, so between the two there was no date the dialog could submit and a discharged resident's funds became unreturnable through the product. And the webhook reactivation toast promised queued deliveries resume when deactivation had cancelled them permanently. **Two quiet arithmetic defects.** `documentHasUnsavedInput` required the new value to be non-empty, so clearing a prefilled field read as nothing to lose and the stale-deployment reload discarded exactly the edit the guard exists to protect; the `instanceof HTMLInputElement` checks went with it, since they do not survive a second realm and made the predicate untestable outside a browser. And `trainingYearWindow` derived its end from the already-clamped start, so a 29 February hire had a one-day hole between consecutive windows -- on 2028-02-28 the current day belonged to no window at all and its hours counted toward nothing. **And one configuration line:** `additional_redirect_urls` carried only the `127.0.0.1` spelling of the dev origin while AGENTS.md documents `localhost`, and GoTrue matches exactly -- so the documented local password-reset flow fell back to the site URL, which is the failure that block exists to prevent. *Closed by:* one migration edited in place, ten frontend files, `supabase/config.toml`, and coverage that fails against what it replaces -- three pgTAP assertions on the census graph, and vitest cases on the settlement bounds, the leap-day windows, the cleared field and the transition graph's shape. |
| J93 | Nine more, from an eighth review round | M | done | **P1-P2, mixed.** Requested on a green head, with the findings-per-round series in the ask -- 3, 9, 2, 5, 8, 12, 10 -- and six named weaknesses rather than a general read. Nine came back, all nine real. **The worst was mine, made in the previous round, and it is the second round running that has been true.** J92 replaced a one-edge blocklist in `transition_resident_census` with a transition graph, and I wrote the graph as a statement about STATUS while the function is also where two BED operations live. `active -> active` with a different `p_bed_id` is the room transfer the function maps to its own `room_transfer` event; `discharged -> discharged` is the bed-release repair `AdmissionOperations.tsx` offers by name, for residents the old bare-discharge path left holding a bed. My `active` arm listed only other statuses and my terminal states had no outgoing edges at all, so both raised 22023 and neither has another route in the product. Every arm now keeps the resident's own status, and that is safe because the guard which refuses same status AND same bed predates the graph -- it is what makes a self-edge always an operation on the bed. Seven of the eight new pgTAP assertions fail against the graph I shipped last round. **A guard whose widening let its own limit hide the answer.** `scim-provision` matched candidate profiles with `ilike(userName).limit(100)` and redid the exact comparison in memory, behind a comment arguing that over-matching is harmless -- which is true only without the limit. `_` and `%` are wildcards the SCIM userName grammar permits, so a crafted userName pushes the protected profile past the hundredth row, this guard reports no candidate, and `resolve_scim_link_profile_id` -- which matches exactly -- goes on to re-role or deactivate that very profile. The wildcards are escaped now; `*` deliberately is NOT, because PostgREST rewrites it to `%` without honouring a backslash (verified against the running stack: `ilike.a\*b@x.com` returns nothing at all), so escaping it would turn a rare over-match into a guaranteed miss. The residual is closed where it matters: a full page is refused rather than filtered, because a truncated set cannot answer this question. **A grant that raised a cap nobody decided to raise.** `additional_attempts_granted` was an integer on `course_assignments` and `enforce_quiz_attempt_cap` added it to the cap of EVERY quiz block under that assignment; nothing constrains a version to one quiz block, so a retry on a failed final assessment quietly raised the limit on module quizzes the learner had passed. The column was introduced by this branch, so it is reshaped rather than patched around: a jsonb map keyed by quiz id, bounded by the same 0-20 rule as a `strict` jsonpath check, and the RPC gained `p_quiz_id` (the two-argument form dropped rather than left beside it) with the quiz checked against the assignment's own course version. The manager's dialog names the assessment. **Two reads that answered from an incomplete set.** The personal-funds statement -- the itemised artifact 55 Pa. Code 2600.20 / 2800.20 asks for, printed to be signed and filed -- was built from the workspace's unbounded newest-first transaction list, which PostgREST truncates at `db-max-rows` (1000, verified on the running stack, and the hosted default). Past that, a statement covering older history printed only the movements that survived truncation and opened on whichever predecessor did -- and because the running-balance column is the ledger's own `balance_after` rather than a re-sum, its reconciliation check could still tick. It reads the period now, plus the entry immediately before it, pages, and says so on the page when even that is incomplete. And the dead-letter replay queue capped at the 50 newest while `replay_integration_webhook_delivery` deliberately leaves the replayed row dead-lettered -- so the page never drained and every older failure was unreachable from the only surface that can replay one. **Three halves of earlier fixes.** `verify_corrective_action` coalesced `v_action.completed_date` ahead of `p_completed_on`, so the date a verifier typed into the field J92 made mandatory was discarded with a success response, leaving a date nobody chose as the survey evidence. `ViolationDetail.tsx` gated the verify control on `canEdit`, which already excludes a completed action -- making its own `|| !verification_notes` arm unreachable and leaving no route to verification on that page at all, where the incident and inspection surfaces gate on `canManage` and reach it. And `useAnnouncements` applied its organization filter only when one was known, so for an org-less platform admin -- the one caller `org_announcements_visible` lets read across tenants -- the J74 interleaving was untouched. **And one mirror missing half its predicate:** the notification retry control copied the RPC's six-hour quarantine and not its `no provider event` clause, so a delivery that later received one still offered a button the server refuses. |
| J94 | Eight more, from a ninth review round | M | done | **P1-P2, mixed.** The round the owner asked for before merging, and it earned the wait: eight findings, all eight real, and **four of them in round eight's own fixes** -- the third round running to find a defect in the round before it. **Two P1s in the census graph J93 wrote.** The self-edges were justified on the reasoning that the pre-existing no-op guard refuses same status AND same bed. It does not: `p_bed_id is not distinct from v.bed_id` is FALSE when the caller sends no bed and the resident holds one, so `temporarily_out -> temporarily_out` with a null bed passed the graph, changed nothing, and still wrote a `resident_census_events` row -- append-only evidence a DHS inspector reads, carrying a movement that never happened. The arms now state the bed condition that makes each self-edge an operation (an `active` transfer naming a DIFFERENT bed; a terminal repair only while a bed is still attached), and the guard is a second line rather than the argument. The second: `discharge_date` was stamped `pa_today()` for any discharged/deceased target, so the bed-release repair J93 restored -- which by construction happens whenever somebody notices the stuck bed, days or months later -- rewrote the historical discharge date on the face sheet and every report keyed on it. A terminal self-edge keeps the stored date now, filling it only when the legacy row never carried one. **A P1 in the SCIM guard J93 touched.** `apply_scim_change` targets `coalesce(resolve_scim_link_profile_id(...), v_link.profile_id)` and keeps that last arm deliberately, so a subject whose employee row was detached and whose address no longer matches still has a target -- while `governedProfileCandidates` selected only `employee_id` and returned NO candidates, which the guard reads as nothing to protect. A suspend or deprovision then deactivated the recorded profile unjudged, including one since promoted to `platform_admin`, the single role no SCIM payload can even assert. The candidate set now carries a `link_fallback` resolution, judged exactly when the two arms above it are empty. **A regression that made a page worse than before I touched it.** J93 disabled the announcements query without an organization, on the reading that a platform admin has none -- but their tenant comes from the header's "Viewing as" picker, which lives in `useViewingOrg` and never reaches `user.organizationId`. So the query was disabled for ever for the one caller it was written for, on a page that told them to use a picker it did not read. It takes `viewingOrgId ?? user.organizationId` now, as EvidenceRoom and QualifiedWorkforce already did. **A second picker the graph never reached:** Admission Operations listed all five census statuses for every resident, so a discharged resident opened for bed repair could be sent to `active` and every such submission ended in a 22023. **A contract asserted rather than enforced:** the column comment says `additional_quiz_attempts` is written only by its RPC, and `protect_course_assignment_fields` restored only `status` and `completed_at` -- so a manager could set twenty attempts on every quiz by hand, with no reason, no version check, no audit row and no notification. The trigger carries the column now and the RPC writes through the transaction-local escape hatch the other trusted RPCs use, opened and closed around the one statement. **And two labels that named the wrong thing:** the grant notification still said "the final assessment" after the grant became per-quiz, and the clinical chart named an EHR chosen by an unordered `limit(1)` while `queue_clinical_observation_writeback` picks the newest `mapped_at` -- so the tooltip and toast could name a different destination than the write. The hook mirrors the RPC's order now and says "the connected EHR" rather than guessing when the newest timestamp is shared, because the RPC's own `limit 1` has no tie-break either. |
| J95 | The post-deploy drift gate failed on a migration that had just deployed cleanly | S | done | **P1, Tooling / deployment.** The #494 merge deployed: CI on main went green, `supabase db push` applied all 24 pending migrations, and production now carries 678 of 678 at `20260906290000`. The step AFTER that, `check-migration-drift.mjs`, then failed and took the workflow with it -- so the edge-function deploy, the prune, the presence check and the content stamp were all skipped. Production ran the new schema with the previous functions, which is the safe direction by design ("functions never run ahead of the schema they need") but is not a finished deploy. **The migration was fine; the checker was wrong.** `supabaseStatementsJoined` reproduces the form the Supabase CLI records in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations.statements`, and it walks the statements libpg_query reports. A comment block AFTER the last statement is not a statement, so the loop walked past it -- while the CLI records it as its own row, exactly as it keeps a comment sitting in FRONT of one. `20260906230000` ends with a five-line note about why it grants nothing to `anon`, so our joined form came to 6,291 characters against the 6,725 recorded: the difference is that 433-character note plus its joining newline, and nothing else. Measured, not inferred -- the fixed function now hashes to `4e431587d308d7562d8e66cb9cfd799f`, byte-identical to what production recorded at apply time. The tail is appended the same way the inter-statement gap already was, and a self-test fixture pins it (it fails against the previous implementation, verified by reverting the fix and keeping the fixture). The change can only ever turn a mismatch into a match -- `localContentHashes` returns candidates and any one matching counts -- so no version that passed before can start failing. **Not allowlisted, deliberately:** the allowlist is for reviewed reconciliation artifacts where the deployed SQL genuinely differs from the file, and here it does not differ at all; recording a checker bug there would have hidden the same false positive for every future migration whose file ends in prose. **It had already hidden it twice.** With the fix in place the checker reports that two existing allowlist entries -- `20260802080000` and `20260804200000`, both files ending in a closing note -- now content-match on their own; each was recorded as a "post-deploy edit to comments or formatting only", which was never true of either file. Nobody edited them after deploying; the checker was short by their closing note, and an exemption was written instead of a diagnosis. Verified end to end against the live project rather than argued: the previous implementation still exits 1 on `20260906230000`, and this one exits 0 with 678 of 678 present and every content hash matching. |
| J96 | Thirty-nine migrations exempted from a gate that already passes them | S | done | **P3, Tooling / deployment.** `scripts/migration-content-allowlist.json` carried 106 reviewed divergences from the 2026-07-24 PT-051 reconciliation. Both selected every `profiles` row and indexed the result, and PostgREST caps an unbounded select at `db-max-rows` -- 1000, verified on the running stack, which already truncates its own 1,221 `audit_logs`. Past a thousand profiles the map loses its tail and both pages render the miss as "Unknown user": an audit row attributed to nobody, which is what an unattributed action looks like to a surveyor. The security-audit one is the worse of the two and said so in its own comment -- "platform_admin has unrestricted profiles SELECT via RLS, so no filtering needed" -- because unrestricted is exactly what makes the cap an installation-wide profile count rather than a per-tenant one. Both now key on the ids actually on screen, which is also strictly cheaper. **And one the automated review found in this pass's own fix, which is the finding that matters most here.** Scoping the two actor-name maps to the caller's ids removes the `db-max-rows` cap on the RESPONSE by moving the bound to the REQUEST -- and one caller has no bound to give. `useListSupportTickets` pages until exhausted and `SupportTickets` renders every row, so the id list is unbounded: a single `.in()` over 5,000 requesters builds a ~190 KB request line (measured, not estimated -- a uuid plus separator is 39 bytes), which a gateway answers with 414 URI Too Long. No caller surfaces this query's error, so the queue would have labelled every requester "Unknown" -- the same symptom the id-scoping was written to remove, arriving through the fix instead of the bug. The bound now lives in the hook rather than in each caller: ids are batched at 100 (~3.9 KB, under the ~8 KB a Kong/nginx front end takes by default), fetched in parallel and merged. **The three copies are now one.** `useProfileNameMap` existed twice -- in `useSecurityAuditLog.ts` and again inside `AuditLog.tsx` -- which is the same duplication that produced the Recents/Header split above, and it is why the unbounded select survived in one copy while the other was being reasoned about; it lives in `hooks/useProfiles.ts` now with all four call sites on it. Nine vitest cases cover the batching, the merge across batches, and the empty case; the request-size assertion fails against an unchunked implementation, checked by running it with the chunk size raised. **Four moderate dependency advisories, one of them reachable.** `fflate@0.8.2` (GHSA-px8p-9vwx-vf98, `unzipSync` infinite-loops on a malformed ZIP64) was pinned by five edge functions, and `accept-learning-package` calls `unzipSync` on the bytes of a tenant-uploaded learning package -- authenticated, but attacker-controlled, which is the advisory's own threat model. Bumped to 0.8.3 in the functions, in `deno.lock` and in the frontend's direct dependency. `postcss` needed its existing override widened for the third time in this file's history (GHSA-fxqj-rqcc-2cmp reaches `<=8.5.22`, which was the override's own previous target), and `qs` took a new one for two advisories that both reach express's resolved 6.15.3. `pnpm audit` and `check:dependencies` are both clean now, where the gate had been passing them because it only fails on high/critical. **And a stale review surface:** Appendix D of the go-live readiness plan still listed `clinical_disclosure_allowed` as "N2 -- pin" nine days after `20260904060000` pinned it, and did not list `pa_local`; it now matches what the advisors actually return. *Not fixed, and stated rather than left implied.* Three things. The other 64 unbounded reads found by the same scan are RLS-scoped list queries where truncation shortens a list rather than misattributing a record, and rewriting all of them is a larger change than this pass should make unasked. The 31 `multiple_permissive_policies` advisor warnings are performance-only and already triaged as N4; the appendix listed thirty of them and left the thirty-first as "one more reported past the first thirty in the raw output", which is now named (`training_classes`) off a full local run. And `go_live_readiness_repairs.test.sql`'s B3 assertion -- "every organization has an organization_settings row" -- is a whole-table invariant, so any run that leaves a settings-less org behind turns it red and names the product rather than whatever created the org. The browser fixtures do exactly that: all four insert into `organizations` directly with the service role, bypassing `record_organization_signup`, which is the only thing that writes the settings row. CI never sees it because it runs `supabase test db` before the browser suite; run them the other way round against one stack, as this pass did, and B3 fails on fourteen orgs the journeys created. **Attempted and reverted:** having the fixtures insert the settings row alongside the org fails with 42501 -- `organization_settings` grants INSERT to `authenticated` and `postgres` and deliberately not to `service_role`. That grant gap is not a production defect (no edge function writes the table at all; the app writes it as the signed-in admin, and signup writes it through a definer), so the remaining fix would be to route four fixtures through `record_organization_signup` -- a rewrite of how every e2e tenant is provisioned, which is more than this finding is worth and is left recorded rather than done. *Environment note:* the Supabase edge-runtime container cannot start in the review sandbox (an OCI rlimit the sandbox forbids), so one browser journey -- the one waiting on `generate-incident-state-form-pdf` to write `state_form_pdf_storage_path` -- could not run; the other 52 pass. |
| J98 | CareBase has no safe entry point to the central CareMetric Support Hub | S | done | **P2, Help / shared learning.** The header Help menu and the existing `/app/help` / `/me/help` Help Center now offer the first-party central Hub behind `support.central_hub`, registered `off` for a controlled rollout. Both use an absolute link to `https://support-hub-web-production.up.railway.app/help`, closing the localhost-relative-link failure, and send only `product=carebase` plus an optional route template selected from `APP_PAGES`; record identifiers, ticket ids, queries, fragments, search terms, users and organizations never enter the URL. The build refuses blank or configured origins other than the approved live Railway origin, and the browser adapter independently fails closed. Existing CareBase FAQs, job aides, manual, glossary, Help Copilot, ticket creation/history/detail routes, platform support queues, courses and HeyGen-backed course records are unchanged and remain available even while the flag is loading, disabled or errored. Closed by `20260907143902_register_central_support_hub_release.sql`, `centralHelp.ts`, the two additive UI links, and unit/pgTAP coverage. |
